\bm{\psi}_{t}-\bm{\psi}_{t-1}=(\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star})-(\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star})=-\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t}+\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t-1}.

(20)

Combining (
18
), (
20
) and (
19
), we find

\begin{split}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}&=\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t}+\gamma_{t}m_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})+\gamma_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})-\beta_{2}(\bm{\psi}_{t}-\bm{\psi}_{t-1})\\
&=\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t}-\gamma_{t}\mathbb{H}_{t}\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t}+\beta_{2}(\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t}-\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t-1})+\gamma_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})\\
&=((1+\beta_{2})I_{d}-\gamma_{t}\mathbb{H}_{t})\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t}-\beta_{2}\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t-1}+\gamma_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t}).\end{split}

(21)

Now for any
k\leq t
, let us define

\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k}^{(t+1)}:=\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{k}.

(22)

Then we have that
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}^{(t+1)}=\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}
. Using the lookahead from (
SGDM
), we have

\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t}=\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}-\bm{\psi}_{t}=(\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{t})-\beta_{1}\left(\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t-1}\right)=\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{(t+1)}-\beta_{1}\left(\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t-1}\right).

(23)

However also note that

\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t-1}=-(\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{t})+(\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{t-1})=-\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{(t+1)}+\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}^{(t+1)}.

(24)

Combining (
23
) and (
24
), we find

\widetilde{\bm{\psi}}_{t}=\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{(t+1)}-\beta_{1}\left(-\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{(t+1)}+\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}^{(t+1)}\right)=(1+\beta_{1})\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{(t+1)}-\beta_{1}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}^{(t+1)}.

(25)

Plugging in (
25
) into (
21
), we obtain the following:

\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}^{(t+1)}=\mathbb{J}_{t}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{(t+1)}+\mathbb{K}_{t}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}^{(t+1)}+L\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-2}^{(t+1)}+\gamma_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t}),

(26)

where

\begin{split}&\mathbb{J}_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}(1+\beta_{1})(1+\beta_{2})I_{d}-\gamma_{t}(1+\beta_{1})\mathbb{H}_{t}\\
&\mathbb{K}_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}-(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\beta_{1}\beta_{2})I_{d}+\gamma_{t}\beta_{1}\mathbb{H}_{t}\\
&L\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\beta_{1}\beta_{2}.\end{split}

(27)

Since we have that
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta
and
\beta_{1}\beta_{2}=0
, we can simplify this as

\begin{split}&\mathbb{J}_{t}=(1+\beta)I_{d}-\gamma_{t}(1+\beta_{1})\mathbb{H}_{t}\\
&\mathbb{K}_{t}=-\beta I_{d}+\gamma_{t}\beta_{1}\mathbb{H}_{t}\\
&L=0.\end{split}

(28)

This gives us a recursive bound based on an augmented shifted-error state. That is, it is written in a coordinate system relative to the minimizer
\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}
. We now convert this into a recursion for the tracking error
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}
. Recall that the minimizer drift is defined as
\Delta_{t}=\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}
. Then we can write

\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{(t+1)}=\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{t}=(\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{t})+(\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\star})=\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\Delta_{t}.

(29)

Similarly we have

\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}^{(t+1)}=(\bm{\theta}_{t-1}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{t-1})+(\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\star}+\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{t-1}^{\star})=\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}-(\Delta_{t-1}+\Delta_{t}).

(30)

Plugging (
29
) and (
30
) into (
26
), we get

\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}=\mathbb{J}_{t}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}+\mathbb{K}_{t}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}+\mathbb{b}_{t}+\gamma_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t}),

(31)

where

\mathbb{b}_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}-(\bm{J}_{t}+\bm{K}_{t})\Delta_{t}-\bm{K}_{t}\Delta_{t-1}=-(I_{d}-\gamma_{t}\mathbb{}H_{t})\Delta_{t}-\mathbb{K}_{t}\Delta_{t-1}.

(32)

It follows that we can write the extended recursion relation as:

\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}\\
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\mathbb{z}_{t+1}}=\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{J}_{t}&\mathbb{K}_{t}\\
I_{d}&0\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\mathbb{B}_{t}}\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\\
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}\end{bmatrix}}_{{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\mathbb{z}_{t}}}+\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{b}_{t}\\
0\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\mathbb{u}_{t}}+\gamma_{t}\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})\\
0\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\bm{\eta}_{t+1}}.

(33)

Note that we can write
\mathbb{B}_{t}=\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{M}_{t}
where

\mathbb{P}=\begin{bmatrix}(1+\beta)I_{d}&-\beta I_{d}\\
I_{d}&0\end{bmatrix},\;\;\mathbb{M}_{t}=\begin{bmatrix}\gamma_{t}(1+\beta_{1})\mathbb{H}_{t}&-\gamma_{t}\beta_{1}\mathbb{H}_{t}\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}.

(34)

Now
\mathbb{P}
has the following eigenvalue decomposition
\mathbb{P}=\mathbb{V}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{V}^{-1}
where

\mathbb{V}=\begin{bmatrix}I_{d}&-\beta I_{d}\\
I_{d}&-I_{d}\end{bmatrix},\;\;\mathbb{V}^{-1}=\frac{1}{1-\beta}\begin{bmatrix}I_{d}&-\beta I_{d}\\
I_{d}&-I_{d}\end{bmatrix},\;\;\mathbb{D}=\begin{bmatrix}I_{d}&0\\
0&\beta I_{d}\end{bmatrix}.

(35)

Define the transformed "mode-splitting" coordinates as follows:

\mathbb{y}_{t}:=\mathbb{V}^{-1}\mathbb{z}_{t}=\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\\
\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{1-\beta}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\beta\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}\\
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}\end{bmatrix}.

(36)

Using this transform with (
31
) gives us:

\begin{split}\mathbb{y}_{t+1}&=\mathbb{V}^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{t}\mathbb{V}\mathbb{y}_{t}+\mathbb{V}^{-1}\mathbb{u}_{t}+\gamma_{t}\mathbb{V}^{-1}\bm{\eta}_{t+1}\\
&=\underbrace{(\mathbb{D}-\mathbb{V}^{-1}\mathbb{M}_{t}\mathbb{V})}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}}\mathbb{y}_{t}+\mathbb{V}^{-1}\mathbb{u}_{t}+\gamma_{t}\mathbb{V}^{-1}\bm{\eta}_{t+1}.\end{split}

(37)

Finally we note that

\mathbb{D}-\mathbb{V}^{-1}\mathbb{M}_{t}\mathbb{V}=\begin{bmatrix}I_{d}-\frac{\gamma_{t}}{1-\beta}\mathbb{H}_{t}&\frac{\gamma_{t}\beta^{\prime}}{1-\beta}\mathbb{H}_{t}\\
-\frac{\gamma_{t}}{1-\beta}\mathbb{H}_{t}&\beta I_{d}+\frac{\gamma_{t}\beta^{\prime}}{1-\beta}\mathbb{H}_{t}\end{bmatrix},\;\;\mathbb{V}^{-1}\bm{\eta}_{t+1}=\frac{1}{1-\beta}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})\\
\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})\end{bmatrix}.

(38)

This concludes the proof.
∎

In order to establish a convergence guarantee of (
SGDM
), we will need to analyze the dynamics of the norm of the extended state vectors. What we aim to do is show a path-wise inequality (analogous to a Lyapunov stability function) of the form
\mathbb{s}_{t}\leq\bm{\Gamma}_{t}\mathbb{s}_{t}
and then get a uniform
\bm{\Gamma}
by upper bounding
\bm{\Gamma}_{t}
,
\forall t\geq 0
.

Corollary C.3

(Uniform stability for (
SGDM
))
.

Let
2.2
,
2.3
hold, and
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta
and
\beta_{1}\beta_{2}=0
for fixed
\beta\in[0,1)
. Consider (
SGDM
) and the extended 2D recursion (
13
). Then when the step-sizes
\gamma_{t}
satisfies

\gamma_{t}\leq\frac{\mu(1-\beta)^{2}}{4L^{2}}

(39)

it holds that the mean-square values of the transformed error vectors evolve according to the recursive inequality below:

\begin{bmatrix}\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}\|^{2}\\
\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}\|^{2}\end{bmatrix}\leq\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}\\
\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}\end{bmatrix},

(40)

where

a=1-\frac{\gamma_{t}\mu}{1-\beta},\;\;b=\frac{\gamma_{t}{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{\mu(1-\beta)},\;\;c=\frac{2\gamma_{t}^{2}L^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{3}},\;\;d=\beta+\frac{2\gamma_{t}^{2}{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{3}}

(41)

and the coefficient matrix is stable i.e.

\rho\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\right)<1.

(42)

Proof of
Corollary

C.3
.

Define the energy vector as follows:

\mathbb{s}_{t}=\begin{bmatrix}\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}\\
\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{2}.

(43)

We will show the pathwise inequality
\mathbb{s}_{t+1}\leq\bm{\Gamma}_{t}\mathbb{s}_{t}
with explicit scalar entries
a_{t},b_{t},c_{t},d_{t}
, and then get a uniform
\bm{\Gamma}
by upper bounded
\bm{\Gamma}_{t}
over
\forall t\geq 0
. From
Lemma

C.2
, we find the first row can be written as

\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}=\mathbb{A}_{t}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}+\mathbb{B}_{t}\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t},

(44)

with
\mathbb{A}_{t}=I_{d}-\eta_{t}\mathbb{H}_{t}
and
\mathbb{B}_{t}=\eta_{t}\beta^{\prime}\mathbb{H}_{t}
where
\eta_{t}=\gamma_{t}/(1-\beta)
and
\beta^{\prime}=\beta\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}
. By Young’s inequality, for
\tau>0

\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\tau}\|\mathbb{A}_{t}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\tau}\|\mathbb{B}_{t}\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}\leq\frac{\|\mathbb{A}_{t}\|^{2}}{1-\tau}\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{\|\mathbb{B}_{t}\|^{2}}{\tau}\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}.

(45)

We will first impose that
\eta_{t}\leq 1/L
. Also recall that since
\mu I_{d}\preceq\nabla^{2}F_{t+1}(\bm{\theta})\preceq LI_{d}
, we immediately have that
\mu I_{d}\preceq\mathbb{H}_{t}\preceq LI_{d}
. Since we have defined
\mathbb{A}_{t}=I_{d}-\eta_{t}\mathbb{H}_{t}
, these facts imply the eigenvalues of
\mathbb{A}_{t}
lie in
[1-\eta_{t}L,1-\eta_{t}\mu]\subset[0,1-\eta_{t}\mu]
. Hence we find that
\|\mathbb{A}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1-\eta_{t}\mu
. Likewise we find that
\|\mathbb{B}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta_{t}\beta^{\prime}L
. Thus we get

\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}\|^{2}\leq\frac{(1-\eta_{t}\mu)^{2}}{1-\tau}\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{t}^{2}{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{\tau}\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}.

(46)

Take
\tau=\eta_{t}\mu\in(0,1)
(indeed this is true since
\eta_{t}\leq 1/L
and
\mu\leq L
). Then we find that

\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}\|^{2}\leq\underbrace{(1-\eta_{t}\mu)}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;a}\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}+\underbrace{\left(\eta_{t}\frac{{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{\mu}\right)}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;b}\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}.

(47)

We will now repeat this for the second row. We can write the second row as

\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}=-\eta_{t}\mathbb{H}_{t}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}+(\beta I_{d}+\eta_{t}\beta^{\prime}\mathbb{H}_{t})\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}=\beta\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}+r_{t},

(48)

where
r_{t}:=\eta_{t}\mathbb{H}_{t}(\beta^{\prime}\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t})
. By the convexity of the map
x\mapsto\|x\|^{2}
, we have that

\begin{split}\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}\|^{2}&=\|\beta\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}+r_{t}\|^{2}\\
&=\|\beta\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}+(1-\beta)r_{t}/(1-\beta)^{2}\|^{2}\\
&\leq\beta\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{1-\beta}\|r_{t}\|^{2}.\end{split}

(49)

Now we bound
\|r_{t}\|
:

\begin{split}\|r_{t}\|\leq\eta_{t}\|\mathbb{H}_{t}\|\|\beta^{\prime}\cdot\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|&\leq\eta_{t}L\left(\beta^{\prime}\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|+\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|\right).\end{split}

(50)

Using the inequality
(a+b)^{2}\lesssim a^{2}+b^{2}
, we conclude that

\begin{split}\|r_{t}\|^{2}\leq 2\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}\left({\beta^{\prime}}^{2}\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}+\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}\right).\end{split}

(51)

Therefore we find that

\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}\|^{2}\leq\underbrace{\left(\frac{2\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\right)}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;c}\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}+\underbrace{\left(\beta+\frac{2\eta_{t}^{2}{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\right)}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;d}\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}.

(52)

Collecting (
47
) and (
52
), we find

\begin{bmatrix}\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}\|^{2}\\
\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}\|^{2}\end{bmatrix}\leq\bm{\Gamma}_{t}\begin{bmatrix}\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}\\
\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}\end{bmatrix},

(53)

with

\bm{\Gamma}_{t}:=\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}1-\eta_{t}\mu&\eta_{t}\frac{{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{\mu}\\
\frac{2\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}&\beta+\frac{2\eta_{t}^{2}{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\end{bmatrix}.

(54)

We will now examine the stability of the 2x2 coefficient matrix
\bm{\Gamma}_{t}
. First assume a constant upper bound

\eta:=\sup_{t}\eta_{t}=\sup_{t}\frac{\gamma_{t}}{1-\beta}.

Define the uniform dominating matrix

\bm{\Gamma}:=\begin{bmatrix}1-\eta\mu&\eta\frac{{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{\mu}\\
\frac{2\eta^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}&\beta+\frac{2\eta^{2}{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\end{bmatrix}.

(55)

Then
\bm{\Gamma}_{t}\leq\bm{\Gamma}
entrywise
\forall t\geq 0
. Hence we have that
\mathbb{s}_{t+1}\leq\bm{\Gamma}s_{t}\leq\bm{\Gamma}^{\alpha}s_{t-\alpha}
for any
\alpha\leq t
. Since the spectral radius of a matrix is upper bounded by its
1
-norm, we have that
\rho(\bm{\Gamma})\leq\max\{a+c,b+d\}
, it suffices to compute the column sums and impose conditions on
\eta
to ensure that
\rho(\bm{\Gamma})<1
. Computing this, we find

\rho(\bm{\Gamma})\leq\max\left\{1-\eta\mu+\frac{2\eta^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta},\beta+\eta\frac{{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{\mu}+\frac{2\eta^{2}{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\right\}.

(56)

We can choose a step-size
\eta
small enough to satisfy:

\begin{cases}\frac{\eta\mu}{2}>\frac{2\eta^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\\
\frac{\eta{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{\mu}>\frac{2\eta^{2}{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\\
1-\beta>\frac{2\eta{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{\mu},\end{cases}

(57)

which is equivalent to

\eta<\min\left\{\frac{\mu(1-\beta)}{4L^{2}},\frac{1-\beta}{2\mu},\frac{\mu(1-\beta)}{{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}\right\}=\frac{\mu(1-\beta)}{4L^{2}}.

(58)

Converting this back to
\gamma
using the relation
\eta=\gamma/(1-\beta)
, we find

\gamma<\min\left\{\frac{\mu(1-\beta)^{2}}{4L^{2}},\frac{(1-\beta)^{2}}{2\mu},\frac{\mu(1-\beta)^{2}}{{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}\right\}=\frac{\mu(1-\beta)^{2}}{4L^{2}}.

(59)

It then holds that

\rho(\bm{\Gamma})<\max\left\{1-\frac{\gamma\mu}{2(1-\beta)},\beta+\frac{2\gamma L^{2}}{\mu(1-\beta)}\right\}\leq 1.

(60)

In this case,
\bm{\Gamma}
is a stable matrix. This concludes the proof.
∎

From
Lemma

C.2
, we established that we have a recursive relation of the form
\mathbb{y}_{t+1}=\widetilde{\bm{B}}_{t}\mathbb{y}_{t}+\mathbb{V}^{-1}\mathbb{u}_{t}+\gamma_{t}\mathbb{V}^{-1}\mathbb{\eta}_{t+1}
. Define the original state matrices as
\bm{\Phi}(t,s):=\bm{B}_{t-1}\bm{B}_{t-2}\dots\bm{B}_{s}
and the transformed state transition matrices as
\widetilde{\bm{\Phi}}(t,s):=\widetilde{\bm{B}}_{t-1}\widetilde{\bm{B}}_{t-2}\dots\widetilde{\bm{B}}_{s}
for
\forall t>s
and
\widetilde{\bm{\Phi}}(s,s):=I_{d}
. With the above established, we can convert the fact that
\bm{\Gamma}
is stable into a bound on
\widetilde{\bm{\Phi}}
and subsequently
\bm{\Phi}
. We will establish this below:

Corollary C.4

(Conversion from
\widetilde{\bm{\Phi}}
to
\bm{\Phi}
)
.

Let
2.2
,
2.3
hold and

\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta
with
\beta_{1}\beta_{2}=0
for fixed
\beta\in[0,1)
. Consider (
SGDM
) and the extended
2
D recursion (
13
).
Assume
\eta_{t}=\gamma_{t}/(1-\beta)
and

\gamma_{t}\leq\frac{\mu(1-\beta)^{2}}{4L^{2}}.

Let
\bm{\Gamma}_{t}
be defined by

\bm{\Gamma}_{t}=\begin{bmatrix}a_{t}&b_{t}\\
c_{t}&d_{t}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}1-\eta_{t}\mu&\eta_{t}\frac{{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{\mu}\\
\frac{2\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}&\beta+\frac{2\eta_{t}^{2}{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\end{bmatrix},\qquad\beta^{\prime}=\beta\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}.

Define

\bar{\rho}\;:=\;\sup_{t\geq 0}\sqrt{\|\bm{\Gamma}_{t}\|_{1}},\qquad\text{where}\quad\|\bm{\Gamma}_{t}\|_{1}=\max\{a_{t}+c_{t},\ b_{t}+d_{t}\}.

Then
\bar{\rho}<1
and for all
t\geq\alpha
,

\|\bm{\Phi}(t,\alpha)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{4}{1-\beta}\,\bar{\rho}^{\,t-\alpha}.

Proof of
Corollary

C.4
.

First note that
\bm{y}_{t}=\widetilde{\bm{\Phi}}(t,\alpha)\bm{y}_{\alpha}
.
Recall from
Lemma

C.2
that we have

\|\bm{y}_{t}\|^{2}=\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}+\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}=\mathbf{1}^{\top}\bm{s}_{t},

(61)

where
\bm{s}_{t}:=[\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2};\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}]\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
.
In
Corollary

C.3
, we showed that for all
k\geq 0
,

\bm{s}_{k+1}\leq\bm{\Gamma}_{k}\bm{s}_{k}\qquad\text{(entrywise)}.

(62)

Iterating (
62
) from
\alpha
to
t-1
gives

\bm{s}_{t}\leq\bm{\Gamma}_{t-1}\bm{\Gamma}_{t-2}\cdots\bm{\Gamma}_{\alpha}\,\bm{s}_{\alpha}\qquad\text{(entrywise)}.

(63)

Taking
\mathbf{1}^{\top}(\cdot)
and using nonnegativity yields

\|\bm{y}_{t}\|^{2}=\mathbf{1}^{\top}\bm{s}_{t}\leq\mathbf{1}^{\top}\Big(\bm{\Gamma}_{t-1}\cdots\bm{\Gamma}_{\alpha}\Big)\bm{s}_{\alpha}\leq\|\bm{\Gamma}_{t-1}\cdots\bm{\Gamma}_{\alpha}\|_{1}\ \|\bm{s}_{\alpha}\|_{1}.

(64)

Since
\|\bm{s}_{\alpha}\|_{1}=\mathbf{1}^{\top}\bm{s}_{\alpha}=\|\bm{y}_{\alpha}\|^{2}
, we obtain

\|\bm{y}_{t}\|^{2}\leq\|\bm{\Gamma}_{t-1}\cdots\bm{\Gamma}_{\alpha}\|_{1}\ \|\bm{y}_{\alpha}\|^{2}.

(65)

By submultiplicativity of the induced
1
-norm,

\|\bm{\Gamma}_{t-1}\cdots\bm{\Gamma}_{\alpha}\|_{1}\leq\prod_{k=\alpha}^{t-1}\|\bm{\Gamma}_{k}\|_{1}\leq\Big(\sup_{k\geq 0}\|\bm{\Gamma}_{k}\|_{1}\Big)^{t-\alpha}.

(66)

Define

\bar{\rho}\;:=\;\sqrt{\sup_{k\geq 0}\|\bm{\Gamma}_{k}\|_{1}}.

(67)

Combining (
65
) and (
67
) yields

\|\bm{y}_{t}\|\leq\bar{\rho}^{\,t-\alpha}\|\bm{y}_{\alpha}\|.

(68)

Therefore,

\|\widetilde{\bm{\Phi}}(t,\alpha)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\bar{\rho}^{\,t-\alpha}.

(69)

Since we have
\bm{z}_{t}=\bm{V}\bm{y}_{t}
, it follows that

\bm{\Phi}(t,\alpha)=\bm{B}_{t-1}\cdots\bm{B}_{\alpha}=\bm{V}\widetilde{\bm{\Phi}}(t,\alpha)\bm{V}^{-1}.

Hence

\|\bm{\Phi}(t,\alpha)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\bm{V}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\bm{V}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\widetilde{\bm{\Phi}}(t,\alpha)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\bm{V}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\bm{V}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\bar{\rho}^{\,t-\alpha}.

(70)

From (
9
), one can show that

\|\bm{V}[x;y]\|\leq\sqrt{2}(\|x\|+\|y\|)\leq 2\|[x;y]\|
,
hence
\|\bm{V}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2
.
This also implies
\|\bm{V}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2/(1-\beta)
. Plugging into (
70
) yields

\|\bm{\Phi}(t,\alpha)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{4}{1-\beta}\,\bar{\rho}^{\,t-\alpha}.

(71)

It remains to show that
\bar{\rho}<1
. Note that

\|\bm{\Gamma}_{t}\|_{1}=\max\{a_{t}+c_{t},\ b_{t}+d_{t}\},

where

a_{t}+c_{t}=1-\eta_{t}\mu+\frac{2\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\leq 1-\frac{\eta_{t}\mu}{2},\qquad\text{since }\ \frac{2\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\leq\frac{\eta_{t}\mu}{2}\ \text{ under }\ \gamma_{t}\leq\frac{\mu(1-\beta)^{2}}{4L^{2}}.

Moreover, using
{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}\leq 1
,

b_{t}+d_{t}=\beta+\eta_{t}\frac{{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{\mu}+\frac{2\eta_{t}^{2}{\beta^{\prime}}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\leq\beta+\eta_{t}\frac{L^{2}}{\mu}+\frac{2\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}.

With
\eta_{t}=\gamma_{t}/(1-\beta)
and
\gamma_{t}\leq\mu(1-\beta)^{2}/(4L^{2})
, we have

\eta_{t}\frac{L^{2}}{\mu}\leq\frac{1-\beta}{4},\qquad\frac{2\eta_{t}^{2}L^{2}}{1-\beta}\leq\frac{1-\beta}{8},

and therefore

b_{t}+d_{t}\leq\beta+\frac{3}{8}(1-\beta)=\frac{3}{8}+\frac{5}{8}\beta<1.

Consequently
\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\bm{\Gamma}_{t}\|_{1}<1
, and thus
\bar{\rho}<1
by (
67
).
This completes the proof.
∎

In light of these results, we can finally prove an expectation bound on the tracking error of (
SGDM
):

Corollary C.5

(Tracking error bound in expectation for (
SGDM
))
.

Let
2.2
,
2.3
, and
3.1
. Consider (
SGDM
) and the extended 2D recursion (
13
). Then when the step-sizes
\gamma_{t}
satisfies

\gamma_{t}\leq\frac{\mu(1-\beta)^{2}}{4L^{2}},

(72)

the following tracking error bound holds in expectation for SGD with momentum with constant stepsize
\gamma_{t}=\gamma
:

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t+1}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq\frac{48}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\rho^{2(t+1)}\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{48(1+\beta+\gamma L)^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\cdot\frac{\Delta^{2}}{(1-\rho)^{2}}+\frac{48}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{1-\rho^{2}}.

(73)

In particular taking
\rho=1-\gamma\mu/2(1-\beta)
, we obtain:

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t+1}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq\frac{48}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\gamma\mu}{1-\beta}(t+1)\right)\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{192(2+\beta)^{2}}{\gamma^{2}\mu^{2}}\Delta^{2}+\frac{96\sigma^{2}\gamma}{\mu(1-\beta)}.

(74)

Proof of
Corollary

C.5
.

For the analysis, we will denote
C_{\beta}:=4/(1-\beta)
. From
Lemma

C.2
, we showed that (
SGDM
) follows a 2D recursive system as follows:

\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1}\\
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\mathbb{z}_{t+1}}=\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{J}_{t}&\mathbb{K}_{t}\\
I_{d}&0\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\mathbb{B}_{t}}\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\\
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}\end{bmatrix}}_{{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\mathbb{z}_{t}}}+\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{b}_{t}\\
0\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\mathbb{u}_{t}}+\gamma_{t}\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})\\
0\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\bm{\eta}_{t+1}}

(75)

where

\begin{split}&\mathbb{J}_{t}=(1+\beta)I_{d}-\gamma_{t}(1+\beta_{1})\mathbb{H}_{t}\\
&\mathbb{K}_{t}=-\beta I_{d}+\gamma_{t}\beta_{1}\mathbb{H}_{t}\\
&\mathbb{b}_{t}=-(I_{d}-\gamma_{t}\mathbb{}H_{t})\Delta_{t}-\mathbb{K}_{t}\Delta_{t-1}.\end{split}

(76)

Iterating (
75
) gives us the identity:

\mathbb{z}_{t+1}=\bm{\Phi}(t+1,0)\mathbb{z}_{0}+\sum_{k=0}^{t}\bm{\Phi}(t+1,k+1)\mathbb{u}_{k}+\sum_{k=0}^{t}\bm{\Phi}(t+1,k+1)\gamma_{k}\mathbb{\eta}_{k+1}.

(77)

Define the following quantities:

A_{t+1}:=\bm{\Phi}(t+1,0)\mathbb{z}_{0},\;\;D_{t+1}:=\sum_{k=0}^{t}\bm{\Phi}(t+1,k+1)\mathbb{u}_{k},\;\;N_{t+1}:=\sum_{k=0}^{t}\bm{\Phi}(t+1,k+1)\gamma_{k}\bm{\eta}_{k+1}.

(78)

Then we have that

\mathbb{z}_{t+1}=A_{t+1}+D_{t+1}+N_{t+1}.

(79)

Using the inequality
\|a+b+c\|^{2}\leq 3\|a\|^{2}+3\|b\|^{2}+3\|c\|^{2}
, we have:

\mathbb{E}\|\mathbb{z}_{t+1}\|^{2}\leq 3\mathbb{E}\|A_{t+1}\|^{2}+3\mathbb{E}\|D_{t+1}\|^{2}+3\mathbb{E}\|N_{t+1}\|^{2}.

(80)

We will now proceed with bounding each of these quantities.

Bounding
\mathbb{E}\|A_{t+1}\|^{2}
:

From the stability established in
Corollary

C.4
, we have

\|A_{t+1}\|=\|\bm{\Phi}(t+1,0)\mathbb{z}_{0}\|\leq C_{\beta}\rho^{t+1}\|\mathbb{z}_{0}\|.

(81)

Thus we can conclude that
\mathbb{E}\|A_{t+1}\|^{2}\leq C_{\beta}^{2}\rho^{2(t+1)}\|\mathbb{z}_{0}\|^{2}
.

Bounding
\mathbb{E}\|D_{t+1}\|^{2}
:

First simply note that
\|\mathbb{u}_{t}\|=\|\mathbb{b}_{t}\|
. Thus it suffices to bound
\|\mathbb{b}_{t}\|
. By definition, we have that
\mathbb{b}_{t}=-(I_{d}-\gamma_{t}\mathbb{}H_{t})\Delta_{t}-\mathbb{K}_{t}\Delta_{t-1}
. Since
\mu I_{d}\preceq\mathbb{H}_{t}\preceq LI_{d}
and
\gamma_{t}\leq 1/L
, the eigenvalues of
I_{d}-\gamma_{t}\mathbb{H}_{t}
lie in
[1-\gamma_{t}L,1-\gamma_{t}\mu]\subset[0,1]
so we have that
\|I_{d}-\gamma_{t}\mathbb{H}_{t}\|\leq 1
. Also note that since
\mathbb{K}_{t}=-\beta I_{d}+\gamma_{t}\beta_{1}\mathbb{H}_{t}
, we have
\|\mathbb{K}_{t}\|\leq\beta+\gamma_{t}L
. Thus putting everything together, we find

\|\mathbb{b}_{t}\|\leq\|I_{d}-\gamma_{t}\mathbb{H}_{t}\|\|\Delta_{t}\|+\|K_{t}\|\|\Delta_{t-1}\|\leq(1+\beta+\gamma_{t}L)\Delta.

(82)

Using triangle inequality and the bound on
\|\mathbb{b}_{t}\|
, we have

\|D_{t+1}\|^{2}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{t}\|\bm{\Phi}(t+1,k+1)\|^{2}\|\mathbb{b}_{k}\|^{2}\leq C_{\beta}^{2}\Delta^{2}(1+\beta+\gamma_{t}L)^{2}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{t}\rho^{t-k}\right)^{2}.

(83)

Using the fact that
\sum_{j\geq 0}\rho^{j}\leq 1/(1-\rho)
, we conclude that

\mathbb{E}\|D_{t+1}\|^{2}\leq C_{\beta}^{2}(1+\beta+\gamma_{t}L)^{2}\frac{\Delta^{2}}{(1-\rho)^{2}}.

(84)

Bounding
\mathbb{E}\|N_{t+1}\|^{2}
:

We will work with the recursion that
N_{t+1}
satisfies:

N_{t+1}=\mathbb{B}_{t}N_{t}+\gamma_{t}\bm{\eta}_{t+1}

(85)

with
N_{0}=0
. Multiplying by
\mathbb{V}^{-1}
, we find that

n_{t+1}=\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}n_{t}+\gamma_{t}\widetilde{\bm{\eta}}_{t+1}

(86)

where
n_{t+1}=\mathbb{V}^{-1}N_{t+1}
,
\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}=\mathbb{V}^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{t}\mathbb{V}
, and
\widetilde{\bm{\eta}}_{t+1}=\mathbb{V}^{-1}\bm{\eta}_{t+1}
. Now since
\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}n_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
measurable and
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\bm{\eta}}_{t+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]=0
, we have by conditioning and then iterated expectation that

\mathbb{E}[\|n_{t+1}\|^{2}]=\|\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}n_{t}\|^{2}+\gamma_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\bm{\eta}}_{t+1}\|^{2}].

(87)

From the stability established in
Corollary

C.3
, we have that
\|\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}n_{t}\|^{2}\leq\rho\|n_{t}\|^{2}
. Also note that from
Lemma

C.2
, we have an explicit form for
\widetilde{\bm{\eta}}_{t+1}
:

\widetilde{\bm{\eta}}_{t+1}=\frac{1}{1-\beta}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})\\
\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})\end{bmatrix}.

(88)

Under the boundedness assumption (
C.1
), we have that

\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\bm{\eta}}_{t+1}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}.

(89)

Thus we find that

\mathbb{E}[\|n_{t+1}\|^{2}]=\rho\|n_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{2\gamma_{t}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}.

(90)

Unrolling this expression and using the fact that
N_{0}=0
, we find that

\mathbb{E}[\|n_{t+1}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{2\gamma_{t}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}(1-\rho)^{2}}.

(91)

Thus we can conclude with:

\mathbb{E}\|N_{t+1}\|^{2}=\|\mathbb{V}\|^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|n_{t+1}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{8\gamma_{t}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}(1-\rho)^{2}}.

(92)

Combining everything and noting that
\|\bm{\theta}_{t+1}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq\|\mathbb{z}_{t}\|^{2}
, we conclude that

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t+1}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq\frac{48}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\rho^{2(t+1)}\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{48(1+\beta+\gamma L)^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\cdot\frac{\Delta^{2}}{(1-\rho)^{2}}+\frac{24}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{1-\rho^{2}}.

(93)

From
Corollary

C.3
, we can take
\rho=1-\gamma\mu/2(1-\beta)
and use the inequality
(1-a)^{2(t+1)}\leq e^{-2a(t+1)}
to conclude:

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t+1}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq\frac{48}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\gamma\mu}{1-\beta}(t+1)\right)\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{192(2+\beta)^{2}}{\gamma^{2}\mu^{2}}\Delta^{2}+\frac{96\sigma^{2}\gamma}{\mu(1-\beta)}.

(94)

∎

Using
Corollary

C.5
, we can similarly obtain an algorithmic guarantee for (
SGDM
):

Corollary C.6

(Time to reach the asymptotic tracking error in expectation for (
SGDM
))
.

Assume
2.2

2.3
, and
3.1
and let
\beta\in[0,1)
. Consider (
SGDM
). Assume a constant stepsize
\gamma_{t}\equiv\gamma
satisfying
\gamma\leq\mu(1-\beta)^{2}/(4L^{2})
, and set

\rho\;:=\;1-\frac{\gamma\mu}{2(1-\beta)}\in(0,1).

Define the (stepsize-dependent) steady-state tracking error

\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma)=\frac{192(2+\beta)^{2}}{\mu^{2}\gamma^{2}}\,\Delta^{2}+\frac{96}{\mu(1-\beta)}\,\sigma^{2}\gamma,\;\;\;\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}\in\arg\min_{\gamma\in(0,\ \mu(1-\beta)^{2}/(4L^{2})]}\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma),\;\;\;\mathcal{E}_{\beta}:=\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}).

(95)

Then:

1.

(Constant learning rate).

If
\gamma_{t}\equiv\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}
, then for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t+1}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\;\lesssim\;\mathcal{E}_{\beta}\quad\text{after time}\quad t\;\lesssim\;\frac{1-\beta}{\mu\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}}\,\log\left(\frac{\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\beta}}\right).

2.

(Step-decay schedule with momentum restart).

Suppose
\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}<\mu(1-\beta)^{2}/(4L^{2})
(i.e., the minimizer of
\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma)
is not at the stability cap).
Define the epoch stepsizes

\gamma_{0}:=\frac{\mu(1-\beta)^{2}}{4L^{2}},\qquad\gamma_{k}:=\frac{\gamma_{k-1}+\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}}{2}\quad(k\geq 1),\qquad K:=1+\Big\lceil\log_{2}\Big(\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}}\Big)\Big\rceil.

Define epoch lengths

T_{0}:=\Big\lceil\frac{1-\beta}{\mu\gamma_{0}}\log\Big(\frac{2\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{0})}\Big)\Big\rceil,\qquad T_{k}:=\Big\lceil\frac{1-\beta}{\mu\gamma_{k}}\,\log 4\Big\rceil\quad(k\geq 1).

Run (
SGDM
) with constant stepsize
\gamma_{k}
for
T_{k}
iterations in epoch
k
,

restarting the momentum buffer at the start of each epoch

(equivalently, set
v=0
or
\theta_{t_{k}-1}=\theta_{t_{k}}
at epoch boundaries).
Let
T:=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}T_{k}
be the total horizon. Then the final iterate satisfies

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{T}-\bm{\theta}_{T}^{\star}\|^{2}\;\lesssim\;\mathcal{E}_{\beta}\quad\text{after time}\quad T\;\lesssim\;\frac{L^{2}}{\mu^{2}(1-\beta)}\,\log\!\left(\frac{\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\beta}}\right)\;+\;\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\beta}},

up to universal numerical constants.

Proof of
Corollary

C.6
.

Fix any stepsize
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{\max}]
and define

\rho(\gamma):=1-\frac{\gamma\mu}{2(1-\beta)}\in(0,1).

By
Corollary

C.5
with this choice of
\rho
and the inequality

\rho^{2(t+1)}\leq\exp(-\gamma\mu(t+1)/(1-\beta))
, we have for all
t\geq 0
:

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t+1}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\;\leq\;\underbrace{\frac{48}{(1-\beta)^{2}}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\;C_{\beta}}\exp\!\Big(-\frac{\mu\gamma}{1-\beta}(t+1)\Big)\,\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}\;+\;\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma).

(96)

Since the assumptions are
uniform in time
(same
\mu,L,\Delta,\sigma
for all
t
),
the same inequality applies when the method is started at any time
s\geq 0

with initial point
\theta_{s}
and initial minimizer
\bm{\theta}_{s}^{\star}
,

provided the momentum buffer is reset at time
s
.
Concretely, if we set
v_{s}=0
(equivalently
\bm{\theta}_{s-1}=\bm{\theta}_{s}
), and run for
T
steps with constant stepsize
\gamma
,
then (
96
) (with
t
replaced by
T-1
and
(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star})
replaced by
(\bm{\theta}_{s},\bm{\theta}_{s}^{\star})
) yields:

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{s+T}-\bm{\theta}_{s+T}^{\star}\|^{2}\;\leq\;C_{\beta}\exp\!\Big(-\frac{\mu\gamma}{1-\beta}T\Big)\,\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{s}-\bm{\theta}_{s}^{\star}\|^{2}\;+\;\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma).

(97)

Proof of (i).

Apply (
96
) with
\gamma=\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}
:

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{t+1}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq C_{\beta}\exp\!\Big(-\frac{\mu\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}}{1-\beta}(t+1)\Big)\,\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{\beta}.

If

t\;\geq\;\frac{1-\beta}{\mu\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}}\log\Big(\frac{C_{\beta}\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}}{\mathcal{E}_{\beta}}\Big),

then the transient term is at most
\mathcal{E}_{\beta}
and hence the total is at most
2\mathcal{E}_{\beta}
.
Substituting
C_{\beta}=48/(1-\beta)^{2}
yields the stated sufficient condition.

Proof of (ii).

Let
t_{0}:=0
and
t_{k+1}:=t_{k}+T_{k}
, and define the epoch iterates and epoch minimizers

X_{k}:=\bm{\theta}_{t_{k}},\qquad X_{k}^{\star}:=\bm{\theta}_{t_{k}}^{\star}.

By construction, at the start of each epoch
k
we restart the momentum buffer (set
v_{t_{k}}=0
),
so we may apply the one-epoch bound (
97
) with
s=t_{k}
,
T=T_{k}
, and
\gamma=\gamma_{k}
:

\mathbb{E}\|X_{k+1}-X_{k+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\;\leq\;C_{\beta}\exp\!\Big(-\frac{\mu\gamma_{k}}{1-\beta}T_{k}\Big)\,\mathbb{E}\|X_{k}-X_{k}^{\star}\|^{2}\;+\;\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k}).

(98)

Contraction for epochs
k\geq 1
.

For
k\geq 1
, by the definition of
T_{k}
we have

T_{k}\;\geq\;\frac{1-\beta}{\mu\gamma_{k}}\log(4C_{\beta}),

and therefore

C_{\beta}\exp\!\Big(-\frac{\mu\gamma_{k}}{1-\beta}T_{k}\Big)\;\leq\;C_{\beta}\exp(-\log(4C_{\beta}))\;=\;\frac{1}{4}.

Substituting this into (
98
) yields, for all
k\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}\|X_{k+1}-X_{k+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\;\leq\;\frac{1}{4}\,\mathbb{E}\|X_{k}-X_{k}^{\star}\|^{2}\;+\;\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k}).

(99)

Base epoch (
k=0
).

By the definition of
T_{0}
,

T_{0}\;\geq\;\frac{1-\beta}{\mu\gamma_{0}}\log\Big(\frac{2C_{\beta}\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}}{\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{0})}\Big),

so

C_{\beta}\exp\!\Big(-\frac{\mu\gamma_{0}}{1-\beta}T_{0}\Big)\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}\;\leq\;\frac{1}{2}\,\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{0}).

Plugging into (
98
) at
k=0
gives

\mathbb{E}\|X_{1}-X_{1}^{\star}\|^{2}\;\leq\;\frac{1}{2}\,\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{0})\;+\;\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{0})\;=\;\frac{3}{2}\,\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{0})\;\leq\;2\,\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{0}).

(100)

Induction.

We claim that for all
k\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}\|X_{k}-X_{k}^{\star}\|^{2}\;\leq\;2\,\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k-1}).

(101)

The base case
k=1
follows from (
100
).
Assume (
101
) holds for some
k\geq 1
. Applying (
99
) yields

\mathbb{E}\|X_{k+1}-X_{k+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}\cdot 2\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k-1})+\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k})=\frac{1}{2}\,\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k-1})+\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k}).

Since
\gamma_{k}=(\gamma_{k-1}+\gamma_{\beta}^{\star})/2
, we have
\gamma_{k-1}\leq 2\gamma_{k}
.
Write
\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma)=A_{\beta}/\gamma^{2}+B_{\beta}\gamma
with
A_{\beta},B_{\beta}>0
.
For any
\gamma>0
,

\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(2\gamma)=\frac{A_{\beta}}{4\gamma^{2}}+2B_{\beta}\gamma\;\leq\;2\Big(\frac{A_{\beta}}{\gamma^{2}}+B_{\beta}\gamma\Big)=2\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma),

so in particular

\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k-1})\;\leq\;\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(2\gamma_{k})\;\leq\;2\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k}).

Therefore,

\mathbb{E}\|X_{k+1}-X_{k+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot 2\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k})+\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k})=2\,\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{k}),

which proves (
101
) at
k+1
and closes the induction.
Hence
\mathbb{E}\|X_{K}-X_{K}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq 2\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{K-1})
. By the definition of
K
,

\gamma_{K-1}-\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}=\frac{\gamma_{0}-\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}}{2^{K-1}}\leq\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2^{K-1}}\leq\gamma_{\beta}^{\star},\quad\text{so}\quad\gamma_{K-1}\leq 2\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}.

Thus,

\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{K-1})\leq\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(2\gamma_{\beta}^{\star})\leq 2\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{\beta}^{\star})=2\mathcal{E}_{\beta},

and therefore

\mathbb{E}\|\bm{\theta}_{T}-\bm{\theta}_{T}^{\star}\|^{2}=\mathbb{E}\|X_{K}-X_{K}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq 2\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{K-1})\leq 4\mathcal{E}_{\beta}.

Time bound.

Since
\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma_{0})\geq\mathcal{E}_{\beta}
, we have

T_{0}\leq\frac{1-\beta}{\mu\gamma_{0}}\log\Big(\frac{2C_{\beta}\|\theta_{0}-\theta_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}}{\mathcal{E}_{\beta}}\Big)+1.

Moreover, for
k\geq 1
,
\gamma_{k}\geq\gamma_{0}/2^{k+1}
(by the same argument as in the SGD proof), hence

\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\frac{1}{\gamma_{k}}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\frac{2^{k+1}}{\gamma_{0}}\leq\frac{2^{K+1}}{\gamma_{0}}\leq\frac{4}{\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}}.

Therefore,

\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}T_{k}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\Big(\frac{1-\beta}{\mu\gamma_{k}}\log(4C_{\beta})+1\Big)\leq\frac{(1-\beta)\log(4C_{\beta})}{\mu}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\frac{1}{\gamma_{k}}+K\leq\frac{4(1-\beta)\log(4C_{\beta})}{\mu\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}}+K.

Combining with the bound on
T_{0}
yields the stated explicit horizon bound. Finally, in the interior regime (unconstrained minimizer),
\mathcal{E}_{\beta}(\gamma)=A_{\beta}/\gamma^{2}+B_{\beta}\gamma

satisfies
\mathcal{E}_{\beta}=\frac{3}{2}B_{\beta}\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}
,
so

\frac{1-\beta}{\mu\gamma_{\beta}^{\star}}=\frac{1-\beta}{\mu}\cdot\frac{B_{\beta}}{(2/3)\mathcal{E}_{\beta}}=\frac{1-\beta}{\mu}\cdot\frac{96\sigma^{2}/(\mu(1-\beta))}{(2/3)\mathcal{E}_{\beta}}=\frac{144\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\beta}}.

Also
\gamma_{0}=\mu(1-\beta)^{2}/(4L^{2})
implies
\frac{1-\beta}{\mu\gamma_{0}}=\frac{4L^{2}}{\mu^{2}(1-\beta)}
. Substituting yields the final bound on
T
.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs of tracking error bounds with high probability

For this section, we will assume a light-tailed assumption on the gradient noise (
D.1
)

Assumption D.1

(Conditional sub-Gaussian gradient noise along iterates)
.

There exists a constant
\sigma>0
such that for all
t\geq 0
,
\big\|\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\theta}_{t})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}\big\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\sigma
and
\big\|\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}\big\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\sigma\;\;\text{a.s.}

D.1
Proof for SGD high probability tracking error bound

We will prove the following high probability bound holds for the tracking error in (
SGD
):

Theorem D.1

(High probability tracking error bound for (
SGD
))
.

Under
3.2
, for all
t\in[T]
,
\gamma\leq\min\left\{\mu/L^{2},1/L\right\}
, and
\delta\in(0,1)
, the following tracking error bound holds for (
SGD
) with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\|\bm{\theta}_{T}-\bm{\theta}_{T}^{\star}\|^{2}\lesssim\left(1-\frac{\gamma\mu}{2}\right)^{T}\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{\mathfrak{D}_{T}}{\gamma\mu}+\Big(d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}+\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}}{\mu}\Big)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}+\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma}{\mu}+\gamma^{2}\sigma^{2}\max_{t\in[T]}\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{(2)}\Big)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}

where
\mathfrak{D}_{t}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}(1-\gamma\mu/2)^{t-\ell-1}\|\bm{\theta}_{\ell}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{\ell+1}^{\star}\|^{2}
and
\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{(2)}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}(1-\gamma\mu/2)^{2(t-\ell-1)}\|\bm{\theta}_{\ell}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{\ell+1}^{\star}\|^{2}
.

Proof of
Theorem

D.1
.

First recall by
Proposition

C.1
that we have

\begin{split}\|\bm{\theta}_{T}-\bm{\theta}_{T}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq&\left(1-\frac{\gamma\mu}{2}\right)^{T}\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{2\mathfrak{D}_{T}}{\gamma\mu}\\
&+\underbrace{\gamma^{2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{\gamma\mu}{2}\right)^{T-\ell-1}\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}}_{\text{(a)}}+\underbrace{\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{\gamma\mu}{2}\right)^{T-\ell-1}M_{\ell}}_{\text{(b)}}\end{split}

(102)

where
M_{t+1}:=-2\gamma_{t}\langle d_{t}-\gamma_{t}m_{t+1}(\bm{\theta}_{t}),\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\theta}_{t})\rangle
with
d_{t}=\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}
. It remains to bound (a) and (b).

Bounding part (a):

First notice the following equivalence:

\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}=\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]+V_{\ell+1},\;\;V_{\ell+1}:=\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}].

(103)

First we bound
V_{\ell+1}
. Note that
V_{\ell+1}
is a martingale difference sequence (MDS) and sub-exponential with
\|V_{\ell+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\lesssim d\sigma^{2}
(
Lemma

G.1
). Define
\rho:=(1-\gamma\mu/2)
. Then for fixed
t\leq T
, let
Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}:=\gamma^{2}\rho^{t-\ell-1}V_{\ell+1}
. Then we have that
\|Z_{\ell}^{(t)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\lesssim\gamma^{2}\rho^{t-\ell-1}d\sigma^{2}
. We also have the following that hold:

\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\gamma^{4}\rho^{2(t-\ell-1)}d^{2}\sigma^{4}\lesssim\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}}{\mu},\;\;\max_{0\leq\ell\leq T-1}\gamma^{2}\rho^{T-\ell-1}d\sigma^{2}\lesssim d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}.

(104)

By Bernstein’s inequality for sub-exponential (
Lemma

G.5
), we have:

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}Z_{\ell}^{(t)}\geq s\right)\lesssim\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{s^{2}\mu}{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}},\frac{s}{\gamma^{2}d\sigma^{2}}\right\}\right).

(105)

Take
s=(d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}+\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{2}\gamma^{3}}{\mu})\log(2T/\delta)
. Furthermore since we have
\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\sigma
, we have
\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]\leq d\sigma^{2}
(
Lemma

G.4
). Thus we have:

\gamma^{2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\rho^{t-\ell-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]\lesssim\frac{d\sigma^{2}\gamma}{\mu}.

(106)

Combining everything, we obtain the event
\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}}(t)
that with probability at least
1-\delta/2T
,

\gamma^{2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{\gamma\mu}{2}\right)^{t-\ell-1}\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}\lesssim\frac{d\sigma^{2}\gamma}{\mu}+(d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}+\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}}{\mu})\log(2T/\delta).

(107)

A union bound over
t=1,\dots,T
gives the event
\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}}=\cap_{t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}}(t)
with
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}})\geq 1-\delta/2
. It remains to bound (b).

Bounding part (b):

Recall that
M_{\ell+1}:=-2\gamma_{\ell}\langle d_{\ell}-\gamma_{t}m_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell}),\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\rangle
with
d_{\ell}=\bm{\theta}_{\ell}-\bm{\theta}_{\ell+1}^{\star}
. Define
a_{\ell}:=d_{\ell}-\gamma\,m_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})
Since
a_{\ell}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable and
\mathbb{E}[\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]=0
, we have
\mathbb{E}[M_{\ell+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]=0
and thus
(M_{\ell+1})_{\ell\geq 0}
is a martingale difference sequence (MDS).

By
3.2
, for any
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable unit vector
u
,
\|u^{\top}\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\sigma
a.s. Hence, for any
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable vector
v
,
\big\langle v,\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\big\rangle\ \text{is conditionally sub-Gaussian given }\mathcal{F}_{\ell},\quad\|\langle v,\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\rangle\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\sigma\|v\|
. Moreover, by the contraction inequality proved in
Lemma

C.1

(using
\gamma\leq\mu/L^{2}
),
\|a_{\ell}\|^{2}=\|d_{\ell}-\gamma m_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}\leq\|d_{\ell}\|^{2}
. Combining these yields

\|M_{\ell+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq 2\gamma\,\sigma\,\|a_{\ell}\|\leq 2\gamma\,\sigma\,\|d_{\ell}\|.

(108)

Recall
\Delta_{\ell}:=\bm{\theta}_{\ell}^{\star}-\bm{\theta}_{\ell+1}^{\star}
and
e_{\ell}:=\bm{\theta}_{\ell}-\bm{\theta}_{\ell}^{\star}
so that

d_{\ell}=\bm{\theta}_{\ell}-\bm{\theta}_{\ell+1}^{\star}=e_{\ell}+\Delta_{\ell}
.
By the predictability/measurability condition on
\bm{\theta}_{\ell+1}^{\star}
(cf. Remark on measurability of the conditional minimizer),

\Delta_{\ell}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable.

Fix
\rho:=1-\gamma\mu/2\in(0,1)
and define for
t\geq 1

\mathfrak{D}_{t}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\rho^{t-\ell-1}\|\Delta_{\ell}\|^{2},\qquad\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{(2)}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\rho^{2(t-\ell-1)}\|\Delta_{\ell}\|^{2},

with
\mathfrak{D}_{0}=\mathfrak{D}_{0}^{(2)}:=0
.
Define the
adapted, nondecreasing
radius process
(\mathfrak{B}_{t})_{t=0}^{T}
by

\mathfrak{B}_{t}:=\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma\mu}\max_{s\in\{0,1,\dots,t\}}\mathfrak{D}_{s}+\frac{d\sigma^{2}\gamma}{\mu}+\Big(d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}+\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}}{\mu}\Big)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}+\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma}{\mu}+\gamma^{2}\sigma^{2}\max_{s\in\{0,1,\dots,t\}}\mathfrak{D}_{s}^{(2)}\Big)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}.

Since
\Delta_{\ell}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable, each
\mathfrak{D}_{s},\mathfrak{D}_{s}^{(2)}
depends only on
(\Delta_{0},\dots,\Delta_{s-1})
and hence

\mathfrak{B}_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
–measurable. Moreover, the max over
s\leq t
makes
\mathfrak{B}_{t}
nondecreasing in
t
.

Define the stopping time

\tau:=\inf\left\{t\in\{0,1,\dots,T\}:\ \|e_{t}\|^{2}>\mathfrak{B}_{t}\right\}\wedge(T+1).

(109)

Define the stopped increments
M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}:=M_{\ell+1}\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}
.
Since
\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable and
\mathbb{E}[M_{\ell+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]=0
,

\mathbb{E}[M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]=\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}\mathbb{E}[M_{\ell+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]=0,

so
(M_{\ell+1}^{\tau})_{\ell\geq 0}
is also an MDS. On the event
\{\ell<\tau\}
we have
\|e_{\ell}\|^{2}\leq\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}
, hence by
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
,

\|d_{\ell}\|^{2}=\|e_{\ell}+\Delta_{\ell}\|^{2}\leq 2\|e_{\ell}\|^{2}+2\|\Delta_{\ell}\|^{2}\leq 2\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}+2\|\Delta_{\ell}\|^{2}\qquad\text{on }\{\ell<\tau\}.

(110)

Plugging (
110
) into (
108
) yields the localized conditional sub-Gaussian bound:

\|M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq 2\gamma\sigma\,\|d_{\ell}\|\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}\lesssim\gamma\sigma\,\sqrt{\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}+\|\Delta_{\ell}\|^{2}}\ \mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}.

(111)

Fix an evaluation time
t\in\{1,\dots,T\}
and define the deterministic weights
w_{\ell}^{(t)}:=\rho^{t-\ell-1}
for
\ell=0,\dots,t-1
and the weighted stopped increments

Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}:=w_{\ell}^{(t)}\,M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}=\rho^{\,t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau},\qquad\ell=0,\dots,t-1.

Then
(Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)})_{\ell=0}^{t-1}
is an MDS and by (
111
) we have

\|Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\lesssim v_{\ell}^{(t)},\qquad v_{\ell}^{(t)}:=\gamma\sigma\,\rho^{\,t-\ell-1}\sqrt{\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}+\|\Delta_{\ell}\|^{2}}\ \mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}.

Since
\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}
and
\Delta_{\ell}
are
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable,
v_{\ell}^{(t)}
is predictable.
Thus there exists an absolute constant
c>0
such that for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\exp\big(\lambda Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}\big)\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\right]\leq\exp\!\big(c\lambda^{2}(v_{\ell}^{(t)})^{2}\big)\qquad\text{a.s.}

Define
S_{k}^{(t)}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}
and
V_{k}^{(t)}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}(v_{\ell}^{(t)})^{2}
, and

Y_{k}(\lambda):=\exp\!\Big(\lambda S_{k}^{(t)}-c\lambda^{2}V_{k}^{(t)}\Big),\qquad k=0,1,\dots,t.

Then
(Y_{k}(\lambda))_{k=0}^{t}
is a nonnegative
(\mathcal{F}_{k})
–supermartingale, so optional sampling at
\tau\wedge t
(
Lemma

G.6
) gives us

\mathbb{E}\big[Y_{\tau\wedge t}(\lambda)\big]\leq 1.

Since
Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}
already includes
\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}
, we have
S_{\tau\wedge t}^{(t)}=S_{t}^{(t)}
and therefore, for any
s>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\rho^{t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\geq s\right)\leq\exp\!\Big(-\lambda s+c\lambda^{2}V_{t}^{(t)}\Big).

Optimizing over
\lambda
yields

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\rho^{t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\geq s\right)\lesssim\exp\!\left(-\frac{s^{2}}{c\,V_{t}^{(t)}}\right).

(112)

It remains to upper bound
V_{t}^{(t)}
. Using
\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}\leq 1
and that
\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}
is nondecreasing,

V_{t}^{(t)}\lesssim\gamma^{2}\sigma^{2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\rho^{2(t-\ell-1)}\big(\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}+\|\Delta_{\ell}\|^{2}\big)\leq\gamma^{2}\sigma^{2}\left(\mathfrak{B}_{t-1}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\rho^{2k}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\rho^{2(t-\ell-1)}\|\Delta_{\ell}\|^{2}\right).

Since
\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\rho^{2k}\leq\frac{1}{1-\rho^{2}}\leq\frac{2}{\gamma\mu}
, we obtain

V_{t}^{(t)}\lesssim\gamma^{2}\sigma^{2}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{B}_{t-1}}{\gamma\mu}+\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{(2)}\right).

(113)

Plugging (
113
) into (
112
) and choosing
s
so that the RHS equals
\delta/(2T)
,
we obtain the event

\mathcal{E}_{M}(t):=\left\{\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\rho^{t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\lesssim\gamma\sigma\sqrt{\left(\frac{\mathfrak{B}_{t-1}}{\gamma\mu}+\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{(2)}\right)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}}\right\},

holding with
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{M}(t))\geq 1-\delta/(2T)
. A union bound over
t=1,\dots,T
yields

\mathbb{P}(\cap_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{E}_{M}(t))\geq 1-\delta/2
. Finally, applying Young’s inequality to the RHS and using
\mathfrak{B}_{t-1}\leq\mathfrak{B}_{T}
,
we obtain that on
\cap_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{E}_{M}(t)
,

\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\rho^{t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\;\lesssim\;\mathfrak{B}_{t-1}\;+\;\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma}{\mu}\log\frac{2T}{\delta}\;+\;\gamma^{2}\sigma^{2}\,\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{(2)}\log\frac{2T}{\delta},\qquad\forall t\in[T].

(114)

Concluding the bound:

Let
\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}}\cap\bigcap_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{E}_{M}(t)
; then
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\delta
.
Work on
\mathcal{E}
. Combining the bound from
\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}}
for term (a) with
(
114
) for term (b) (and using the stopped recursion from

Lemma

C.1
), we obtain for every
t\in[T]
that

\begin{split}\|e_{t}\|^{2}=\|\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\star}\|^{2}&\lesssim\left(1-\frac{\gamma\mu}{2}\right)^{t}\|e_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma\mu}\,\mathfrak{D}_{t}+\frac{d\sigma^{2}\gamma}{\mu}+\Big(d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}+\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}}{\mu}\Big)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}\\
&\quad+\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma}{\mu}+\gamma^{2}\sigma^{2}\,\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{(2)}\Big)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}.\end{split}

(115)

Using
\left(1-\frac{\gamma\mu}{2}\right)^{t}\|e_{0}\|^{2}\leq\|e_{0}\|^{2}
and the monotonicity of the maxima,

\mathfrak{D}_{t}\leq\max_{s\leq t}\mathfrak{D}_{s}
and
\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{(2)}\leq\max_{s\leq t}\mathfrak{D}_{s}^{(2)}
, the RHS of
(
115
) is bounded by
\mathfrak{B}_{t}
as defined in (
D.1
). Hence, on
\mathcal{E}
,

\|e_{t}\|^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{B}_{t},\qquad\forall t\in[T].

Therefore the event
\{\|e_{t}\|^{2}>\mathfrak{B}_{t}\}
never occurs on
\mathcal{E}
, and by the definition of the stopping time
(
109
) we have
\tau=T+1
on
\mathcal{E}
. Consequently
M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}=M_{\ell+1}
for all

\ell\leq T-1
on
\mathcal{E}
, i.e., the stopped process coincides with the original process. Taking
t=T
above yields

\|e_{T}\|^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{B}_{T}
on
\mathcal{E}
. Since
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\delta
, the claimed high-probability
bound follows.
∎

D.2
Proof for SGDM high probability tracking error bound

Before we prove the (
SGDM
) high probability bound, we will first establish a recursive relation for the (
SGDM
) tracking error that we will use to prove the high probability bound, analogous to
Proposition

C.1
.

Proposition D.1

(Final iterate recursive relation for the (
SGDM
) tracking error)
.

For all
t\geq 0
and fixed
\gamma=\gamma_{t}\leq\min\left\{1/L,\mu(1-\beta)^{2}/4L^{2}\right\}
, the following recursive relation for the tracking error holds provided one takes a zero momentum initialization
\bm{\theta}_{-1}=\bm{\theta}_{0}
:

\|\bm{\theta}_{t+1}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}\|^{2}\lesssim\frac{2}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\tilde{\rho}^{t+1}\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma\mu}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\beta}\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}+\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\psi}_{\ell})\|^{2}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}

where
\tilde{\rho}=1-\eta^{2}\mu^{2}/4
,
\eta=\gamma/(1-\beta)
,
\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}:=\sum_{\ell\leq t}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}\|\mathbb{b}_{\ell}\|^{2}
, and the martingale increment is
M_{\ell+1}:=2\eta\langle\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{\ell}\mathbb{y}_{\ell}+\mathbb{r}_{\ell},\bm{\zeta}_{\ell+1}\rangle
where
\mathbb{b}_{\ell},\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{\ell},\;\mathbb{y}_{\ell},\;\mathbb{r}_{\ell},\;\bm{\zeta}_{\ell+1}
are defined in
Lemma

C.2
.

Proof of
Proposition

D.1
.

Define
\eta:=\gamma/(1-\beta)
. From
Lemma

C.2
, we have
the transformed "mode-splitting" coordinates as follows:

\mathbb{y}_{t}:=\mathbb{V}^{-1}\mathbb{z}_{t}=\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\\
\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{1-\beta}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\beta\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}\\
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}\end{bmatrix}.

(116)

Using this transform with (
31
) gives us:

\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\\
\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\;\mathbb{y}_{t}}=\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}I_{d}-\frac{\gamma_{t}}{1-\beta}\mathbb{H}_{t-1}&\frac{\gamma_{t}\beta^{\prime}}{1-\beta}\mathbb{H}_{t-1}\\
-\frac{\gamma_{t}}{1-\beta}\mathbb{H}_{t-1}&\beta I_{d}+\frac{\gamma_{t}\beta^{\prime}}{1-\beta}\mathbb{H}_{t-1}\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\;\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}}\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}\\
\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}\end{bmatrix}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{1-\beta}\begin{bmatrix}-(I_{d}-\gamma_{t}\mathbb{H}_{t-1})\Delta_{t-1}-\mathbb{K}_{t-1}\Delta_{t-2}\\
-(I_{d}-\gamma_{t}\mathbb{H}_{t-1})\Delta_{t-1}-\mathbb{K}_{t-1}\Delta_{t-2}\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\;\mathbb{r}_{t}}+\underbrace{\frac{\gamma_{t}}{1-\beta}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\xi}_{t}(\bm{\psi}_{t-1})\\
\bm{\xi}_{t}(\bm{\psi}_{t-1})\end{bmatrix}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\;\;\eta\bm{\zeta}_{t+1}}

(117)

where

\beta^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\beta\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}

(118)

\mathbb{H}_{t-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}F_{t+1}(\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}+s(\bm{\psi}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t+1}^{\star}))ds

(119)

\mathbb{K}_{t-1}=-\beta I_{d}+\gamma_{t}\beta_{1}\mathbb{H}_{t-1}.

(120)

Define
E_{t}:=\|\mathbb{y}_{t}\|^{2}=\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}+\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}
. From the momentum initialization, it follows that
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}=(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{0}-\beta\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{0})/(1-\beta)
and
\check{\bm{\theta}}_{0}=0
. This implies that

E_{0}:=\|\mathbb{y}_{0}\|^{2}\leq\frac{2}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{0}\|^{2}

(121)

where the inequality holds from triangle inequality and
\beta\in(0,1)
. Another fact to note is that by definition of
\mathbb{y}_{t}
, one sees that
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}=\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\beta\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}
. This implies

\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}\leq\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}+\beta^{2}\|\check{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\|^{2}\leq E_{t}

(122)

where the first inequality holds from triangle inequality and
\beta\in(0,1)
. Thus it suffices to upper bound
E_{t}
.

From (
117
), we have that

\mathbb{y}_{t+1}=\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}\mathbb{y}_{t}+\mathbb{r}_{t}+\eta\bm{\zeta}_{t+1}.

(123)

Expanding the square, we get

\|\mathbb{y}_{t+1}\|^{2}=\|\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}\mathbb{y}_{t}+\mathbb{r}_{t}\|^{2}+2\eta\langle\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}\mathbb{y}_{t}+\mathbb{r}_{t},\bm{\zeta}_{t+1}\rangle+\eta^{2}\|\bm{\zeta}_{t+1}\|^{2}.

(124)

We will first upper bound
\|\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}\mathbb{y}_{t}+\mathbb{r}_{t}\|^{2}
. Applying Young’s inequality with
\alpha=\eta\mu/2\in(0,1)
, we have

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}\mathbb{y}_{t}+\mathbb{r}_{t}\|^{2}&\leq\left(1+\frac{\eta\mu}{2}\right)\|\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}\mathbb{y}_{t}\|^{2}+\left(1+\frac{2}{\eta\mu}\right)\|\mathbb{r}_{t}\|^{2}\\
&\leq\left(1+\frac{\eta\mu}{2}\right)\rho\|\mathbb{y}_{t}\|^{2}+\left(1+\frac{2}{\eta\mu}\right)\|\mathbb{r}_{t}\|^{2}.\end{split}

(125)

where the last inequality holds from the stability condition (
Corollary

C.3
). Now observe that

\left(1+\frac{\eta\mu}{2}\right)\rho=\left(1+\frac{\eta\mu}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{\eta\mu}{2}\right)=1-\frac{\eta^{2}\mu^{2}}{4}\in(0,1).

(126)

Now define
M_{t+1}:=2\eta\langle\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}\mathbb{y}_{t}+\mathbb{r}_{t},\bm{\zeta}_{t+1}\rangle
. Since
\mathbb{B}_{t}\mathbb{y}_{t}+\mathbb{r}_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
measurable and
\mathbb{E}[\bm{\zeta}_{t+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]=0
, we see that
M_{t+1}
is a MDS. Furthermore note that since
\|\bm{\zeta}_{t+1}\|^{2}=2\|\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})\|^{2}
, we have

\eta^{2}\|\bm{\zeta}_{t+1}\|^{2}=2\eta^{2}\|\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})\|^{2}.

(127)

Combining everything, we get the following recursive relation:

E_{t+1}\leq\tilde{\rho}E_{t}+\left(1+\frac{2}{\eta\mu}\right)\|\mathbb{r}_{t}\|^{2}+2\eta^{2}\|\bm{\xi}_{t+1}(\bm{\psi}_{t})\|^{2}+M_{t+1}.

(128)

Unrolling this recursion, we find

E_{t+1}\leq\tilde{\rho}^{t}E_{0}+\left(1+\frac{2}{\eta\mu}\right)\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}\|\mathbb{r}_{\ell}\|^{2}+2\eta^{2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\psi}_{\ell})\|^{2}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}.

(129)

We can now simplify the drift term using
\mathbb{r}_{\ell}=[\mathbb{b}_{\ell};\mathbb{b}_{\ell}]/(1-\beta)
(
13
). This gives us

\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}\|\mathbb{r}_{\ell}\|^{2}=\frac{2}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}\|\mathbb{r}_{\ell}\|^{2}:=\frac{2}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}.

(130)

We can also use
\eta=\gamma/(1-\beta)
and (
121
) to get

E_{t+1}\leq\frac{2}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\tilde{\rho}^{t}\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{0}\|^{2}+\left(1+\frac{2}{\eta\mu}\right)\frac{2}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}+\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\psi}_{\ell})\|^{2}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{t}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}.

(131)

Since
1+2/\eta\mu\lesssim 1/\eta\mu
, we have

\left(1+\frac{2}{\eta\mu}\right)\frac{2}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}\lesssim\frac{1-\beta}{\gamma\mu}\frac{1}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}=\frac{1}{\gamma\mu}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\beta}\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}.

(132)

This lets us conclude using (
122
).
∎

We can now establish a high-probability bound on the tracking error for (
SGDM
). This proof will be similar to
Theorem

3.5
.

Theorem D.2

(High probability tracking error bound for (
SGDM
))
.

Under
3.2
, for all
T\in[T]
,
\gamma\leq\min\left\{1/L,\ \mu(1-\beta)^{2}/(4L^{2})\right\}
, and
\delta\in(0,1)
, provided one takes a zero momentum initialization
\bm{\theta}_{-1}=\bm{\theta}_{0}
, the following tracking error bound holds for (
SGDM
) with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\begin{split}\|\bm{\theta}_{T}-\bm{\theta}_{T}^{\star}\|^{2}\lesssim\;&\frac{2}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\gamma^{2}\mu^{2}}{4(1-\beta)^{2}}T\right)\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma\mu}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\beta}\,\mathfrak{D}_{T}^{\mathrm{mom}}+\frac{d\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\\
&+\left(\frac{d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}+\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}}{\mu(1-\beta)^{3}}\right)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}+\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\,\mathfrak{D}_{T}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}\right)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}.\end{split}

where
\tilde{\rho}:=1-\frac{\gamma^{2}\mu^{2}}{4(1-\beta)^{2}}
and

\mathfrak{D}_{T}^{\mathrm{mom}}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,T-\ell-1}\|\bm{b}_{\ell}\|^{2},\qquad\mathfrak{D}_{T}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,2(T-\ell-1)}\|\bm{b}_{\ell}\|^{2},

with
\bm{b}_{\ell}:=-(I_{d}-\gamma\bm{H}_{\ell-1})\Delta_{\ell-1}-\bm{K}_{\ell-1}\Delta_{\ell-2}
and
\bm{H}_{\ell},\bm{K}_{\ell}
defined as in
Lemma

C.2
.

Proof of
Theorem

D.2
.

First recall by
Proposition

D.1
that we have,

\|\bm{\theta}_{T}-\bm{\theta}_{T}^{\star}\|^{2}\lesssim\frac{2}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\tilde{\rho}^{T}\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma\mu}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\beta}\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}+\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\tilde{\rho}^{T-\ell-1}\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\psi_{\ell})\|^{2}}_{\text{(a)}}+\underbrace{\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\tilde{\rho}^{T-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}}_{\text{(b)}}

(133)

where
\tilde{\rho}=1-\eta^{2}\mu^{2}/4
,
\eta=\gamma/(1-\beta)
,
\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}:=\sum_{\ell\leq T-1}\tilde{\rho}^{T-\ell-1}\|\mathbb{b}_{\ell}\|^{2}
, and the martingale increment is
M_{\ell+1}:=2\eta\langle\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{\ell}\mathbb{y}_{\ell}+\mathbb{r}_{\ell},\bm{\zeta}_{\ell+1}\rangle
where
\mathbb{b}_{\ell},\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{\ell},\;\mathbb{y}_{\ell},\;\mathbb{r}_{\ell},\;\bm{\zeta}_{\ell+1}
are defined in
Lemma

C.2
. It remains to bound (a) and (b).

Bounding part (a):

First notice the following equivalence:

\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}=\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]+V_{\ell+1},\;\;V_{\ell+1}:=\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}].

(134)

First we bound
V_{\ell+1}
. Note that
V_{\ell+1}
is a martingale difference sequence (MDS) and sub-exponential with
\|V_{\ell+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\lesssim d\sigma^{2}
(
Lemma

G.1
). Then for fixed
t\leq T
, let
Z_{\ell}^{(t)}:=2\gamma^{2}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}V_{\ell}/(1-\beta)^{2}
. Then we have that
\|Z_{\ell}^{(t)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\lesssim 2\gamma^{2}\tilde{\rho}^{t-\ell-1}d\sigma^{2}/(1-\beta)^{2}
. We also have the following that hold:

\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\frac{2\gamma^{4}}{(1-\beta)^{4}}\tilde{\rho}^{2(T-\ell-1)}d\sigma^{2}\lesssim\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}}{\mu(1-\beta)^{3}},\;\;\max_{0\leq\ell\leq T-1}\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\tilde{\rho}^{T-\ell-1}d\sigma^{2}\lesssim\frac{d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}.

(135)

By Bernstein’s inequality for sub-exponential (
Lemma

G.5
), we have:

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}Z_{\ell}^{(t)}\geq s\right)\lesssim\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{s^{2}\mu(1-\beta)^{3}}{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}},\frac{s(1-\beta)^{2}}{d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}\right\}\right).

(136)

Take
s=(d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}/(1-\beta)^{2})\log(T/2\delta)+(d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}/\mu(1-\beta)^{3})\log(T/2\delta)
. Then with probability at least
1-\delta/2T
, we have

\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}Z_{\ell}^{(t)}\lesssim\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}d\sigma^{2}\right)\log(2T/\delta)+\left(\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}}{\mu(1-\beta)^{3}}\right)\log(2T/\delta).

(137)

Furthermore since we have
\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\sigma
, we have
\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})^{2}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]\leq d\sigma^{2}
(
Lemma

G.4
). Thus we have:

\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{T-\ell-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell}(\bm{\theta}_{t})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell-1}]\lesssim\frac{\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}(1-\rho)}d\sigma^{2}.

(138)

Since
1-\tilde{\rho}=\eta^{2}\mu^{2}/4=\gamma^{2}\mu^{2}/4(1-\beta)^{2}
, we have

\frac{\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{(1-\rho)}\asymp\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}.

(139)

Combining everything, we obtain the event
\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}}(t)
that with probability at least
1-\delta/2T
,

\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-1}\tilde{\rho}^{T-\ell-1}\|\bm{\xi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\psi}_{\ell})\|^{2}\lesssim\frac{d\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}+\left(\frac{d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}+\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}}{\mu(1-\beta)^{3}}\right)\log(2T/\delta).

(140)

A union bound over
t=1,\dots,T
gives the event
\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}}=\cap_{t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}}(t)
with
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}})\geq 1-\delta/2
. It remains to bound (b).

Bounding part (b):

Recall that
M_{\ell+1}:=2\eta\langle\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{\ell}\mathbb{y}_{\ell}+\mathbb{r}_{\ell},\bm{\zeta}_{\ell+1}\rangle
where
\mathbb{b}_{\ell},\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{\ell},\;\mathbb{y}_{\ell},\;\mathbb{r}_{\ell},\;\bm{\zeta}_{\ell+1}
are defined in
Lemma

C.2
. Define
a_{\ell}:=\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{\ell}\mathbb{y}_{\ell}+\mathbb{r}_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
. Since
a_{\ell}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable and
\mathbb{E}[\bm{\zeta}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]=0
, we have
\mathbb{E}[M_{\ell+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]=0
and thus
(M_{\ell+1})_{\ell\geq 0}
is a martingale difference sequence (MDS).

By
3.2
, for any
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable unit vector
u
,
\|u^{\top}\bm{\zeta}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\sigma
a.s. Hence, for any
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable vector
v
,
\big\langle v,\bm{\zeta}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\big\rangle\ \text{is conditionally sub-Gaussian given }\mathcal{F}_{\ell},\quad\|\langle v,\bm{\zeta}_{\ell+1}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell})\rangle\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\sigma\|v\|
. This yields

\|M_{\ell+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq 2\eta\,\sigma\,\|a_{\ell}\|.

(141)

Since
\|a_{\ell}\|
depends on
\bm{y}_{\ell}
, which is random and can, a priori, be unbounded, we cannot directly apply a uniform sub-Gaussian martingale concentration inequality.
We therefore localize via a predictable (time-varying) stopping radius, which remains valid under stochastic drift.

Recall
\eta:=\gamma/(1-\beta)
and

\tilde{\rho}:=1-\frac{\eta^{2}\mu^{2}}{4}=1-\frac{\gamma^{2}\mu^{2}}{4(1-\beta)^{2}}\in(0,1).

Define for
t\geq 1
the drift-forcing aggregates

\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,t-\ell-1}\|\bm{b}_{\ell}\|^{2},\qquad\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,2(t-\ell-1)}\|\bm{b}_{\ell}\|^{2},

with
\mathfrak{D}_{0}^{\mathrm{mom}}=\mathfrak{D}_{0}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}:=0
.
Define the adapted, nondecreasing radius process
(\mathfrak{B}_{t})_{t=0}^{T}
by

\begin{split}\mathfrak{B}_{t}:=&\frac{2}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma\mu}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\beta}\max_{s\in\{0,1,\dots,t\}}\mathfrak{D}_{s}^{\mathrm{mom}}+\frac{d\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\\
&+\left(\frac{d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}+\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}}{\mu(1-\beta)^{3}}\right)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}+\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\max_{s\in\{0,1,\dots,t\}}\mathfrak{D}_{s}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}\right)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}.\end{split}

(142)

Since each
\bm{b}_{\ell}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable (it depends only on predictable matrices and the drift increments up to time
\ell
),

\mathfrak{B}_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
–measurable and nondecreasing in
t
.

Define the stopping time

\tau:=\inf\left\{t\in\{0,1,\dots,T\}:\ \|\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\star}\|^{2}>\mathfrak{B}_{t}\right\}\wedge(T+1).

(143)

Define the stopped increments
M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}:=M_{\ell+1}\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}
.
Since
\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
–measurable and
\mathbb{E}[M_{\ell+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]=0
,

\mathbb{E}[M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]=\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}\mathbb{E}[M_{\ell+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]=0,

(144)

so
(M_{\ell+1}^{\tau})_{\ell\geq 0}
is also an MDS.

On the event
\{\ell<\tau\}
we have
\|e_{\ell}\|^{2}\leq\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}
and also
\|e_{\ell-1}\|^{2}\leq\mathfrak{B}_{\ell-1}\leq\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}
.
Moreover, by the definition of the
2d
-state
\bm{y}_{\ell}
in
Lemma

C.2
,

\|\bm{y}_{\ell}\|^{2}\lesssim\|e_{\ell}\|^{2}+\|e_{\ell-1}\|^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}\qquad\text{on }\{\ell<\tau\}.

(145)

Hence, using
\|a_{\ell}\|=\|\widetilde{\bm{B}}_{\ell}\bm{y}_{\ell}+\bm{r}_{\ell}\|\leq\|\widetilde{\bm{B}}_{\ell}\bm{y}_{\ell}\|+\|\bm{r}_{\ell}\|\lesssim\|\bm{y}_{\ell}\|+\|\bm{r}_{\ell}\|
,
we obtain on
\{\ell<\tau\}
that

\|a_{\ell}\|\lesssim\sqrt{\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}}+\|\bm{r}_{\ell}\|.

Plugging into (
141
) yields the localized
\Psi_{2}
bound

\|M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\lesssim 2\eta\sigma\big(\sqrt{\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}}+\|\bm{r}_{\ell}\|\big)\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}.

(146)

Fix an evaluation time
t\in\{1,\dots,T\}
.
Define the deterministic weights
w_{\ell}^{(t)}:=\tilde{\rho}^{\,t-\ell-1}
for
\ell=0,\dots,t-1
and the weighted stopped increments

Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}:=w_{\ell}^{(t)}\,M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}=\tilde{\rho}^{\,t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau},\qquad\ell=0,\dots,t-1.

By (
144
) and the fact that
w_{\ell}^{(t)}
is deterministic,

\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]=0
, i.e.,
(Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)})_{\ell=0}^{t-1}
is an MDS.
Let
S_{k}^{(t)}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}
denote its partial sums (
k=0,1,\dots,t
). Define the predictable scale

v_{\ell}^{(t)}:=\eta\sigma\,\tilde{\rho}^{\,t-\ell-1}\big(\sqrt{\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}}+\|\bm{r}_{\ell}\|\big)\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\},\qquad\ell=0,\dots,t-1,

so that (
146
) implies
\|Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq v_{\ell}^{(t)}
.
A standard implication of conditional
\Psi_{2}
control is the conditional mgf bound: there exists an absolute constant
c>0
such that for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\exp\big(\lambda Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}\big)\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\right]\leq\exp\!\big(c\lambda^{2}(v_{\ell}^{(t)})^{2}\big)\qquad\text{a.s.}

(147)

Define the predictable variance proxy
V_{k}^{(t)}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}(v_{\ell}^{(t)})^{2}
and the exponential process

Y_{k}(\lambda):=\exp\!\Big(\lambda S_{k}^{(t)}-c\lambda^{2}V_{k}^{(t)}\Big),\qquad k=0,1,\dots,t.

Using (
147
) we have

\mathbb{E}\!\left[Y_{k+1}(\lambda)\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]=Y_{k}(\lambda)\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\exp\!\big(\lambda Z_{k+1}^{(t)}-c\lambda^{2}(v_{k}^{(t)})^{2}\big)\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]\leq Y_{k}(\lambda),

so
(Y_{k}(\lambda))_{k=0}^{t}
is a nonnegative
(\mathcal{F}_{k})
–supermartingale. Now apply optional sampling at the bounded stopping time
\tau\wedge t
(
Lemma

G.6
):

\mathbb{E}\big[Y_{\tau\wedge t}(\lambda)\big]\leq\mathbb{E}[Y_{0}(\lambda)]=1.

(148)

Since
Z_{\ell+1}^{(t)}
already includes the factor
\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}
,
we have
S_{\tau\wedge t}^{(t)}=S_{t}^{(t)}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}
.
Therefore, by Markov’s inequality and (
148
), for any
s>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\geq s\right)\leq\exp\!\Big(-\lambda s+c\lambda^{2}V_{t}^{(t)}\Big).

Optimizing over
\lambda
yields the sub-Gaussian tail

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\geq s\right)\lesssim\exp\!\left(-\frac{s^{2}}{c\,V_{t}^{(t)}}\right).

(149)

By definition of
v_{\ell}^{(t)}
and using
\mathbf{1}\{\ell<\tau\}\leq 1
and monotonicity of
\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}
,

V_{t}^{(t)}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}(v_{\ell}^{(t)})^{2}\lesssim\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,2(t-\ell-1)}\big(\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}+\|\bm{r}_{\ell}\|^{2}\big)\leq\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}\left(\mathfrak{B}_{t-1}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,2k}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,2(t-\ell-1)}\|\bm{r}_{\ell}\|^{2}\right).

Using
\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{2k}\leq\frac{1}{1-\tilde{\rho}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\tilde{\rho}}\asymp\frac{(1-\beta)^{2}}{\gamma^{2}\mu^{2}}
and
\eta=\gamma/(1-\beta)
gives

V_{t}^{(t)}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\,\mathfrak{B}_{t-1}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\,\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,2(t-\ell-1)}\|\bm{r}_{\ell}\|^{2}.

(150)

Moreover, by the linear relation between
\bm{r}_{\ell}
and the drift forcing
\bm{b}_{\ell}
in

Lemma

C.2
, we have
\|\bm{r}_{\ell}\|^{2}\lesssim\|\bm{b}_{\ell}\|^{2}
and thus

\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,2(t-\ell-1)}\|\bm{r}_{\ell}\|^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}.

Plugging into (
150
) yields

V_{t}^{(t)}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\,\mathfrak{B}_{t-1}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\,\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}.

Choose
s>0
so that
\exp\!\left(-s^{2}/c\,V_{t}^{(t)}\right)=\delta/(2T)
. Then with probability at least
1-\delta/(2T)
,

\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\;\lesssim\;\sqrt{V_{t}^{(t)}\,\log\frac{2T}{\delta}}.

(151)

Using the above bound on
V_{t}^{(t)}
and
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
gives

\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\;\lesssim\;\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\,\mathfrak{B}_{t-1}\,\log\frac{2T}{\delta}}+\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}\,\log\frac{2T}{\delta}}.

(152)

Applying Young’s inequality
\sqrt{\mathcal{B}}\,u\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{B}+u^{2}
to the first term with

u:=\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\log\frac{2T}{\delta}}
, and to the second term in the same way, yields

\sum_{\ell=0}^{t-1}\tilde{\rho}^{\,t-\ell-1}M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}\;\lesssim\;\mathfrak{B}_{t-1}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\log\frac{2T}{\delta}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}\,\log\frac{2T}{\delta}.

(153)

Call this event
\mathcal{E}_{M}(t)
. A union bound over
t\in[T]
yields

\mathcal{E}_{M}:=\bigcap_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{E}_{M}(t)\qquad\text{with}\qquad\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{M})\geq 1-\delta/2.

(154)

Concluding the bound:

Let
\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{M}
; then
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\delta
.
Work on
\mathcal{E}
. Combining the bound for (a) from
\mathcal{E}_{\bm{\xi}}
and the bound for (b) from (
153
),
and applying
Proposition

D.1
, we obtain for each
t\in[T]
that

\begin{split}\|\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\star}\|^{2}&\lesssim\frac{2}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\tilde{\rho}^{\,t}\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma\mu}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\beta}\,\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}+\frac{d\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}+\left(\frac{d\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}+\frac{d^{2}\sigma^{4}\gamma^{3}}{\mu(1-\beta)^{3}}\right)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}\\
&+\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(1-\beta)^{2}}\,\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}\right)\log\frac{2T}{\delta}.\end{split}

Using
\tilde{\rho}^{\,t}\leq 1
and
\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom}}\leq\max_{s\leq t}\mathfrak{D}_{s}^{\mathrm{mom}}
,

\mathfrak{D}_{t}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}\leq\max_{s\leq t}\mathfrak{D}_{s}^{\mathrm{mom},(2)}
, the RHS is bounded by
\mathfrak{B}_{t}

as defined in (
142
). Hence on
\mathcal{E}
,

\|\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\star}\|^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{B}_{t},\qquad\forall t\in[T].

Therefore the event
\{\|\bm{\theta}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{t}^{\star}\|^{2}>\mathfrak{B}_{t}\}
never occurs on
\mathcal{E}
,
so by (
143
) we have
\tau=T+1
on
\mathcal{E}
.
Consequently
M_{\ell+1}^{\tau}=M_{\ell+1}
for all
\ell\leq T-1
on
\mathcal{E}
, i.e., the stopped bounds coincide with the original process.
Taking
t=T
yields
\|\bm{\theta}_{T}-\bm{\theta}_{T}^{\star}\|^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{B}_{T}
on
\mathcal{E}
.
Since
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\delta
, the claimed high-probability bound follows.
Finally, note
\tilde{\rho}^{\,T}\leq\exp\!\left(-\frac{\gamma^{2}\mu^{2}}{4(1-\beta)^{2}}T\right)
.

∎

Appendix E
Minimax lower bounds for SGDM under nonstationary strongly-convex losses

Before we proceed with obtaining the minimax lower bound, we must first reduce the problem of lower bounding regret to the problem of lower bounding the success probability of testing a sequence of functions. We do this as there are information theoretic tools such as Fano’s lemma
(
9
)
that be used. We first give the definition that measures the "discrepancy" between two functions
g,\widetilde{g}:\Theta\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
:

\chi(g,\widetilde{g}):=\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta}\max\{g(\bm{\theta})-g^{\star},\widetilde{g}(\bm{\theta})-\widetilde{g}^{\star}\}\;\;\;\text{where}\;\;\;g^{\star}=\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta}g(\bm{\theta}),\;\;\widetilde{g}^{\star}=\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta}\widetilde{g}(\bm{\theta}).

This measures characterizes the best regret one can achieve without knowing whether
g
or
\widetilde{g}
is the underlying function. This allows us to obtain a reduction from regret minimization to testing problems. This is analogous to the reduction used by
(
3
;
7
)
.

E.1
From regret minimization to testing

Fix
1\leq p<\infty
,
1\leq q\leq\infty
, and
V_{T}>0
. Let
\phi^{G}
denote the noisy gradients feedback model. Consider any finite packing
\Theta=\{g^{(1)},\dots,g^{(M)}\}\ \subseteq\ \mathcal{G}_{p,q}(\mathbb{V}_{T})
where each
g^{(i)}=(g^{(i)}_{1},\dots,g^{(i)}_{T})
is a length-
T
sequence of convex losses. The following reduction formalizes that
uniformly small regret
over
\mathcal{G}_{p,q}(\mathbb{V}_{T})
implies the existence of a
hypothesis test
that identifies the true sequence
f\in\Theta
with constant probability. We include a proof for completeness but note that this result is standard in
(
3
;
7
)
.

Lemma E.1

(Reduction from regret to testing)
.

Let
\Theta\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{p,q}(\mathbb{V}_{T})
be finite, and let
\chi(\cdot,\cdot)
be a nonnegative per-round separation functional. Suppose there exists an admissible policy
\pi
such that

\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{p,q}(\mathbb{V}_{T})}\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\pi,\phi^{G}}(g)\;\leq\;\frac{1}{9}\,\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}g,\tilde{g}\in\Theta\\
g\neq\tilde{g}\end{subarray}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\chi(g_{t},\tilde{g}_{t}).

(155)

Then there exists an estimator (measurable decision rule) mapping the interactions and feedback
\{(\bm{\theta}_{t-1},\phi_{t}^{G})\}_{t=1}^{T}\ \longmapsto\ \widehat{g}\in\Theta
such that

\sup_{g\in\Theta}\ \mathbb{P}_{g}\!\left[\widehat{g}\neq g\right]\ \leq\ \frac{1}{3},

(156)

where
\mathbb{P}_{f}
denotes the distribution induced by the policy
\pi
and the feedback model
\phi^{G}
when the underlying function sequence is
f
.

Proof of
Lemma

E.1
.

Fix any admissible policy
\pi
satisfying (
155
). For a realized actions and feedback produced by
\pi
, write the (realized) dynamic regret of
\pi
on a sequence
f
as

\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\pi}(g)\;=\;\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigl(g_{t}(\bm{\theta}_{t-1})-g_{t}^{\star}\bigr),\qquad g_{t}^{\star}:=\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta}g_{t}(\bm{\theta}),

where
\{\bm{\theta}_{t-1}\}_{t=1}^{T}
are the actions generated by
\pi
and the expectation in
\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{p,q}(V_{T})}\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\pi}(g)
is over the feedback noise under
\mathbb{P}_{g}
. Let

\Delta\;:=\;\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}g,\tilde{g}\in\Theta\\
g\neq\tilde{g}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{t=1}^{T}\chi(g_{t},\tilde{g}_{t}).

By the assumption in (
155
), for every
g\in\mathcal{G}_{p,q}(V_{T})
we have

\mathbb{E}_{g}\bigl[\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\pi}(g)\bigr]\ \leq\ \frac{1}{9}\Delta.

Applying Markov’s inequality to the nonnegative random variable
\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\pi}(g)
yields, for every
g\in\Theta
,

\mathbb{P}_{g}\!\left(\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\pi}(g)\ \leq\ \frac{1}{3}\Delta\right)\;\geq\;1-\frac{\mathbb{E}_{g}[\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\pi}(g)]}{\Delta/3}\;\geq\;1-\frac{(1/9)\Delta}{\Delta/3}\;=\;\frac{2}{3}.

(157)

Define the estimator
\widehat{g}
as the empirical risk minimizer over
\Theta
evaluated on the realized action sequence
\{\bm{\theta}_{t-1}\}_{t=1}^{T}
:

\widehat{g}\;\in\;\arg\min_{g\in\Theta}\ \sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigl(g_{t}(\bm{\theta}_{t-1})-g_{t}^{\star}\bigr).

(158)

By definition of
\widehat{g}
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigl(\widehat{g}_{t}(\bm{\theta}_{t-1})-\widehat{g}_{t}^{\star}\bigr)\;\leq\;\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigl(g_{t}(\bm{\theta}_{t-1})-g_{t}^{\star}\bigr)\;=\;\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\pi}(g).

(159)

Now condition on the event
\mathcal{E}_{g}\;:=\;\left\{\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\pi}(g)\ \leq\ \frac{1}{3}\Delta\right\}

which has probability at least
2/3
under
\mathbb{P}_{g}
by (
157
). On
\mathcal{E}_{g}
, we can upper bound the separation between
\widehat{g}
and
g
as follows. First, by the definition of
\chi
assumed in the lemma statement (nonnegative per-round separation),

\chi(\widehat{g}_{t},g_{t})\;\leq\;\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta}\max\Big\{\widehat{g}_{t}(\bm{\theta})-\widehat{g}_{t}^{\star},\ g_{t}(\bm{\theta})-g_{t}^{\star}\Big\}\;\leq\;\max\Big\{\widehat{g}_{t}(\bm{\theta}_{t-1})-\widehat{g}_{t}^{\star},\ g_{t}(\bm{\theta}_{t-1})-g_{t}^{\star}\Big\},

(160)

and hence, summing over
t
and using
\max\{a,b\}\leq a+b
for
a,b\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\chi(\widehat{g}_{t},g_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\max\Big\{\widehat{g}_{t}(\bm{\theta}_{t-1})-\widehat{g}_{t}^{\star},\ g_{t}(\bm{\theta}_{t-1})-g_{t}^{\star}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigl(\widehat{g}_{t}(\bm{\theta}_{t-1})-\widehat{g}_{t}^{\star}\bigr)+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigl(g_{t}(\bm{\theta}_{t-1})-g_{t}^{\star}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigl(g_{t}(\bm{\theta}_{t-1})-g_{t}^{\star}\bigr)\;=\;2\,\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\pi}(g),

(161)

where in the last step we used (
159
). On
\mathcal{E}_{g}
, (
161
) gives

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\chi(\widehat{g}_{t},g_{t})\;\leq\;2\,\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\pi}(g)\;\leq\;\frac{2}{3}\Delta.

(162)

Finally, we show that (
162
) forces
\widehat{g}=g
. Indeed, if
\widehat{g}\neq g
, then by definition of
\Delta
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\chi(\widehat{g}_{t},g_{t})\;\geq\;\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}g,\tilde{g}\in\Theta\\
g\neq\tilde{g}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{t=1}^{T}\chi(g_{t},\tilde{g}_{t})\;=\;\Delta,

which contradicts (
162
). Therefore, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{g}
we must have
\widehat{g}=g
